are statistically equivalent by the fact that
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{y},1}
is a random matrix,
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{y},2}
and
\overline{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{y}}
are random full column-rank matrices, and
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{y}}
is a random vector.

Now consider the function
\mathsf{F}^{\prime}
which takes as input
\mathbf{y}
and outputs
(r_{1},r_{2}):=(\overline{\mathsf{F}}(k_{1},\mathbf{y}),\mathsf{F}(k_{2},\mathbf{y}))
for randomly sampled keys
k_{1},k_{2}
. By an essentially identical argument as in the proof of Lemma
B.2
, we have that
\mathsf{F}^{\prime}
is a puncturable PRF.

We now define the circuits
E_{0}(\mathbf{y})=\mathcal{D}_{0}(\mathbf{y};\mathsf{F}^{\prime}((\overline{k_{\mathsf{lin}}},k_{\mathsf{F}}),\mathbf{y}))
and
E_{1}(\mathbf{y})=\mathcal{D}_{1}(\mathbf{y};\mathsf{F}(k^{\prime}_{\mathsf{F}},\mathbf{y}))
for freshly sampled keys
\overline{k_{\mathsf{lin}}},k_{\mathsf{F}},k^{\prime}_{\mathsf{F}}
.

Observing that the circuit
E_{0}
is only used black-box in
P
,
P^{-1}
, and
D
, Lemma
A.1
implies that switching to
E_{1}
is
(f(\kappa)-\mathsf{poly}(\kappa),O(\frac{2^{r}}{f(\kappa)}))
-computationally indistinguishable.

•

\mathsf{Hyb}_{4}
: Moving the second permutation outside
\overline{P}
and
\overline{P}^{-1}
.

Recall that in the previous hybrid, the second inner permutation
\overline{\Pi}^{-1}(k_{\mathsf{out}},\cdot)
, which acts on
\{0,1\}^{d}
to generate
\mathbf{y}
, is used in
\overline{P}
and
\overline{P}^{-1}
. We simply move the application of the permutation outside of
\overline{P}
and
\overline{P}^{-1}
. This does not change the functionality of
P
,
P^{-1}
, or
D
, so this change is
(f(\kappa),O(\frac{1}{f(\kappa)}))
-computationally indistinguishable by the security of the outer iO scheme.

•

\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}
: Dropping
\overline{P}
and
\overline{P}^{-1}
.

Having moved the permutation
\overline{\Pi}^{-1}(k_{\mathsf{out}},\cdot)
to the outside of
\overline{P}
and
\overline{P}^{-1}
, we observe that
P
,
P^{-1}
, and
D
never use the output of
\overline{P}
and
\overline{P}^{-1}
, so we can simply generate
P
,
P^{-1}
, and
D
without using
\overline{P}
and
\overline{P}^{-1}
at all. This does not change the functionality of
P
,
P^{-1}
, or
D
, so this change is
(f(\kappa),O(\frac{1}{f(\kappa)}))
-computationally indistinguishable by the security of the outer iO scheme.

It is easy to see that the final distribution in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}
is precisely the output distribution of
\widetilde{\mathsf{Setup}}(1^{\lambda},n,r,\ell,s)
, and the outputs of
\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}
and
\mathsf{Hyb}_{5}
are
(f(\kappa)-\mathsf{poly}(\kappa),O(\frac{2^{r}}{f(\kappa)}))
-computationally indistinguishable. By hypothesis, we have that the success probability of
\mathcal{A}
in the final hybrid is
\varepsilon
, and thus we have by the lemma’s assumptions that
\varepsilon_{B}\geq\varepsilon-O\left(\frac{2^{r}}{f(\kappa)}\right)\geq\varepsilon/2
, as desired.
∎

Appendix G
Improved Analysis of the
[
SZ25
]
Distinguisher

In this section, we show that the distinguisher described in
[
SZ25
]
does in fact also succeed in distinguishing between the case where the preimage state has not been measured at all and the case that (only) the first bit of the preimage (or indeed, any given bit) has been measured. Recall that
[
SZ25
]
showed the weaker statement that the distinguisher succeeds in distinguishing between the case where the preimage state has not been measured at all and the case that
all the bits
of the preimage have been measured.

We first recall the construction of
[
SZ25
]
.

Construction 7

: One-Shot Signature Scheme,
[
SZ25
]

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
be the statistical security parameter. Define
s:=16\cdot\lambda
,
r:=s\cdot(\lambda-1)
, and
n:=r+\frac{3}{2}\cdot s
.

Let
\Pi:\{0,1\}^{n}\to\{0,1\}^{n}
be a random permutation and let
F:\{0,1\}^{r}\to\{0,1\}^{n\cdot(n-r+1)}
a random function. Let
H(x)
denote the first
r
output bits of
\Pi(x)
, and
J(x)
denote the remaining
n-r
bits, which are interpreted as a vector in
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{n-r}
. For each
\mathbf{y}\in\{0,1\}^{r}
, let
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{y}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{n\times(n-r)}
be a random matrix with full column-rank, and
\mathbf{b}_{\mathbf{y}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{n}
be uniformly random, both are generated by the output randomness of
F(\mathbf{y})
.

Then, we let
\mathcal{P}:\{0,1\}^{n}\to(\{0,1\}^{r}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{n})
,
\mathcal{P}^{-1}:(\{0,1\}^{r}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{n})\to\{0,1\}^{n}\cup\{\bot\}
,
\mathcal{D}:(\{0,1\}^{r}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{n})\to\{0,1\}
be the following oracles:

•

\mathcal{P}(x)=(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot J(x)+\mathbf{b}_{\mathbf{y}})
where
\mathbf{y}=H(x)

•

\mathcal{P}^{-1}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{u})=\begin{cases}\Pi^{-1}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z})&\exists\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{n-r}\text{ such that }\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{b}_{\mathbf{y}}=\mathbf{u}\\
\bot&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

•

\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{v})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }\mathbf{v}^{T}\cdot\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{y}}=0^{n-r}\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We denote the above distribution of oracles by
\mathcal{O}_{n,r}
. We define our hash function as
H
, which can be easily computed by querying
\mathcal{P}
, considering only the first
r
bits and discarding the second output.

Lemma G.1
.

For a given security parameter
\lambda
, let
\mathcal{O}_{n,r}:=\mathcal{O}_{n(\lambda),r(\lambda)}
be the oracle distribution of Construction
.

There exists a pair of
\mathsf{QPT}
algorithms
(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{D})
such that for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle{\scriptstyle\absolutevalue{\Pr[{\scriptstyle\calD(\ket*{\psi_{y, b}}\!, \aux) = 1:} \begin{array}{r} {\scriptstyle(\calP, \calP^{-1}, \calD) \gets\calO_{n, r}} \\ {\scriptstyle(\ket*{\psi}, \aux) \gets\calS^{(\calP, \calP^{-1}, \calD)}} \\ {\scriptstyle\ket*{\psi_{y, b}} \gets\Measure_{H, 1}(\ket*{\psi})} \end{array}] -
\Pr[{\scriptstyle\calD(\ket*{\psi_y}\!, \aux) = 1:} \begin{array}{r} {\scriptstyle(\calP, \calP^{-1}, \calD) \gets\calO_{n, r}} \\ {\scriptstyle(\ket*{\psi}, \aux) \gets\calS^{(\calP, \calP^{-1}, \calD)}} \\ {\scriptstyle\ket*{\psi_y} \gets\Measure_{H}}(\ket*{\psi}) \end{array}]}}\geq\frac{1}{4}.

Here,
\ket*{\psi_{y, b}}\leftarrow\mathsf{Measure}_{H,1}(\ket*{\psi})
means to measure the first bit of
\ket*{\psi}
in the computational basis after measuring
H^{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{-1},\mathcal{D})}(\cdot)
, resulting in the outcome
(b,y)
, while
\ket*{\psi_y}\leftarrow\mathsf{Measure}_{H}
means to measure
H^{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{-1},\mathcal{D})}(\cdot)
, resulting in outcome
y
. Then the state
\ket*{\psi}
collapses to
\ket*{\psi_y}
, which contains some superposition of preimages of
y
.

Proof.

\mathcal{S}_{H}
computes a uniform superposition
\ket{+}^{\otimes n}
and outputs it as
\ket{\psi}
with no auxiliary information.

\mathcal{D}_{H}
, given an unknown
n
-qubit state
\ket{\phi}:=\sum_{x}\alpha_{x}\ket{x}
, acts as follows:

1.

Compute
\mathcal{P}
to obtain the state

\sum_{x}\alpha_{x}\ket{x}\ket{\vecy_x}\ket*{\matA_\vecy\cdot\vecw_x + \vecb_\vecy}.

2.

Apply
\mathcal{P}^{-1}
to uncompute the input register holding
x
to obtain the state

\sum_{x}\alpha_{x}\ket{\vecy_x}\ket*{\matA_\vecy\cdot\vecw_x + \vecb_\vecy}.

3.

Apply
H^{\otimes n}
to the rightmost register (which contains coset vectors) to obtain the state

\sum_{x}\alpha_{x}\ket{\vecy_x}(H^{\otimes n}\ket*{\matA_\vecy\cdot\vecw_x + \vecb_\vecy}).

4.

Apply
\mathcal{D}(\cdot,\cdot)
before measuring and returning the output bit register.

The case where just
H
is measured is the same as in
[
SZ25
]
:
\mathcal{D}_{H}
receives the state
\sum_{x:H(x)=y}\ket{x}
for some
y
, and after the second step
\mathcal{D}_{H}
holds the state

\ket{y}\sum_{x:H(x)=y}\ket*{\matA_\vecy\cdot\vecw_x + \vecb_\vecy}.

Since applying the QFT to a uniform superposition over a coset yields a uniform superposition over the dual subspace (with a phase), it follows that
\mathcal{D}_{H}
outputs 1 with probability 1.

In the case where both
H
and the first preimage bit is measured,
\mathcal{D}_{H}
receives the state
\sum_{x:H(x)=y,x_{1}=b}\ket{x}
for some hash
y
and bit
b
. Define the set
S_{y,b}=\{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{x}+\mathbf{b}_{\mathbf{y}}\}_{\mathbf{w}_{x}:x_{1}=b}\subseteq\{\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{x}+\mathbf{b}_{\mathbf{y}}\}_{\mathbf{w}_{x}\in\{0,1\}^{n-r}}
. At the end of the second step,
\mathcal{D}_{H}
holds the state

\frac{1}{\sqrt{|S_{y,b}|}}\ket{y}\otimes\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in S_{y,b}}\ket*{\vecs}.

After applying the QFT
H^{\otimes n}
,
\mathcal{D}_{H}
holds the state

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{n}\cdot|S_{y,b}|}}\ket{y}\otimes\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{n}}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in S_{y,b}}(-1)^{\mathbf{s}\cdot\mathbf{z}}\right)\ket{\vecz}.

Thus, the probability of step 4 outputting 1 is precisely

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n}\cdot|S_{y,b}|}\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{y}}^{\perp}}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in S_{y,b}}(-1)^{\mathbf{s}\cdot\mathbf{z}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n}\cdot|S_{y,b}|}\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{y}}^{\perp}}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in S_{y,b}}(-1)^{\mathbf{b}_{\mathbf{y}}\cdot\mathbf{z}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n}\cdot|S_{y,b}|}\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{y}}^{\perp}}|S_{y,b}|^{2}=\frac{2^{r}\cdot|S_{y,b}|^{2}}{2^{n}\cdot|S_{y,b}|}=\frac{|S_{y,b}|}{2^{n-r}}.

If we fix a permutation
\Pi
, we know that each hash value
y
is equally likely to occur, and conditioned on
\Pi
and
y
, the outcome
b
will be measured with probability
\frac{|S_{y,b}|}{2^{n-r}}
. Thus, the probability that
\mathcal{D}_{H}
outputs 1 is precisely

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Pi,y}\left[\frac{|S_{y,0}|^{2}}{2^{2(n-r)}}+\frac{|S_{y,1}|^{2}}{2^{2(n-r)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\Pi,y}\left[\frac{|S_{y,0}|^{2}+(2^{n-r}-|S_{y,0}|)^{2}}{2^{2(n-r)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\Pi,y}\left[\frac{2|S_{y,0}|^{2}+2^{2(n-r)}-2^{n-r+1}|S_{y,0}|}{2^{2(n-r)}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\Pi,y}\left[\frac{2|S_{y,0}|^{2}}{2^{2(n-r)}}\right]+\frac{2^{2(n-r)}-2^{n-r+1}\cdot 2^{n-r-1}}{2^{2(n-r)}}

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{\Pi,y}\left[\left(\frac{|S_{y,0}|}{2^{n-r}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=2\left[\left(\frac{2^{n-r-1}}{2^{n-r}}\right)^{2}+\frac{2^{n-r}}{2^{2(n-r)}}\cdot\frac{2^{n-1}}{2^{n}}\cdot\frac{2^{n}-2^{n-1}}{2^{n}}\cdot\frac{2^{n}-2^{n-r}}{2^{n}-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2^{n-r+1}}\cdot\frac{2^{n}-2^{n-r}}{2^{n}-1}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2^{n-r+1}}\leq\frac{3}{4},

since
|S_{y,0}|
is a hypergeometric variable.

We conclude that the distinguishing advantage of
(\mathcal{S}_{H},\mathcal{D}_{H})
is at least
1-\frac{3}{4}=\frac{1}{4}
, as desired.
∎
